Thus gradient function:
```
F=fft(a,m); b=ifft(F*F).real[:L]; bmax=max; v=(b-bmax)/tau; w=softmax(v); W=fft(w,m); c=ifft(W*conj(F)).real[:n];

Line search: Given v, we can compute a from soft. We can try step directions. Since a normalized, adding to v. We can perform backtracking:
- grad_norm = sqrt(mean(grad^2))
- d = -grad/grad_norm (descent for F? Need c - mean increases high? We want reduce max b. c_i is derivative of bmax? If a_i where c high, increasing a_i increases max. So gradient ascent? We want minimize bmax, so direction = -c_i? In log variable, derivative dF/dv_i = a_i c_i (plus). So descent dv = -lr * a_i(c_i - mean). - Try candidate: v_trial = v - step * dataset. Score. If not better, reduce step. But computing score each line search FFT; can do few.

Let's implement `optimize_seq(a, time_limit, seed, verbose)` returning best normalized and score.
Features:
- Use time limit, seed.
- n up to maybe 16384. m next pow2(2n-1) maybe for 16384 m=65536? FFT 65536 each iter. OK maybe 5000 iters in 100s? 65536 FFT ~0.003s? In Python maybe 0.005, 5000 ~25s plus overhead. Good.
- Use `np.float64`.
- Initialize v = log(a). Clip to [-50,50]. v -= mean.
- Keep best_a, best_score.
- Use state Adam: m_v, v_v, t.
- Step base: Maybe use **Adaptive Learning Rate** based on score improvement. Start lr=0.2? previous 0.1. We can tune. Log space steps clipped ±0.75. Use momentum beta1=0.9,beta2=0.999 or 0.001? For non-smooth, maybe lower beta2 0.95 for responsiveness.
- Soft tau: We can choose tau as fraction of bmax. Need scale. b values sum S^2=1, peak ~0.75/n. For n=4096, peak ~1.8e-4. Tau should be ~ peak*0.05? Previous tau_frac=0.03 to0.18 of bmax. That's 2.5-18% spread. Could tune: tau = bmax * max(0.02, 0.1*(1-progress)+0.02). Too small -> active single peak; too large -> smooth. We can randomize.
- Use both **top-lag weight** and **hat weight**: w = softmax((b-bmax)/tau). Additionally, add uniform weight over top K? If max is multi-lags, softmax may concentrate too little. We can blend: w = (1-α)softmax + α/topK * indicator(topK). α maybe 0.2. Previous added 0.5 to top5 and renorm. We can use α tune.
- Also use **active set exact**: occasionally compute c for equal weights on lags where b > bmax*(1-δ) (δ e.g. 0.01) and take a step. This strongly flatten plateau. But if too many, direction broad.
- **Restarts**: Track no improvement for `stuck_time`. When stuck, either add noise to current v (scale) or restart from best with random perturbation. We can maintain a list of elite sequences? Maybe just best.
- **Periodic perturbations in Fourier domain**: Add random low-frequency modulation: multiply a by 1+ρ cos(2π r t+φ), renorm. This may escape local minima. Since objective sensitive to density shape. Use ρ decays.
- **Spectral target restarts** inside optimize? Maybe separate.

Could also implement **coarse-to-fine annealing**: For a stage n, run optimize for some time. Then interpolate best to larger n, perhaps also add small random smooth perturbation. The coarser optimized shape guides. This can be effective.

What lengths? Suppose time 2400. We can spend:
- 0-120s: simple parameter search / quick at n=1024 from multiple inits (including previous) to find good shape.
- 120-360s: n=2048 optimize 2-4 candidates.
- 360-960s: n=4096 optimize from best and a few variants.
- 960-1920s: n=8192 optimize.
- 1920-2360s: n=16384 maybe short. Return best. Exact validation final length ≤8192 maybe.

   ```
   F = fft(a)
   for it:
      Fmag_target = sqrt(abs(FFT(target)))
      phase = F/abs(F)
      F = lambda*F + (1-lambda)*Fmag_target*phase
      a_new = ifft(F).real[:n]
      pos = max(a_new,0); blend; if score improve update.
   ```

Spectral factorization with target trapezoid:
```
def spectral_target_factor(target, a0, n, m, iters, blend):
    T = fft(target, m)
    F = fft(a0, m)
    for it:
        # Newton magnitude update
        # Option 1: Fnew = 0.5*(F + T/F)
        # Option 2: phase preserve with target magnitude
        Fabs = np.abs(F)+eps
        phase = F/Fabs
        Tmag = np.sqrt(np.abs(T)+eps)
        Fc = phase * Tmag
        F = (1-λ)*F + λ*Fc
        anew = ifft(F).real[:n]
        pos = max(anew,0); normalize; blend with old.
        periodic score.
```

Let's design a robust **target pursuit** function:
```
def target_pursuit(a, max_time, seed, target=None, score_goal=1.5):
    best = a, score
    for outer in ...:
        b,bmax=...
        if score(best) <= goal: break
        # choose M_target
        if target is None:  # trapezoid based on desired sum
            M_target = bmax * 0.99? or lower until score_goal? Could use bmax * (score_goal / score_current) *1.02
        else:
            M_target = bmax
        T = clip_targets(b, M_target, sum1) or shape target
        # project onto T via spectral_factor_target few iterations
        a2, sc2 = spectral_target_factor(T, a, n, iters=50, blend=0.5)
        if sc2 < best: update; else maybe do gradient target.
        # gradient polish: update towards T for K iters
```
But spectral factor per outer might be expensive if many. Use maybe 20 iters.

Let's design `optimize_seq` in detail.

```
def optimize_seq(a0, time_limit, seed, target=None, verbose=True):
    n=len(a0)
    m=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    a=_proj_nonneg_sum(a0)
    v=np.log(a+1e-300)
    v -= mean; v=clip(-30,30)
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.95? maybe; eps=1e-8
    mu=zeros; nu=zeros; t=0
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_fft(a)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_limit
    last_improve=start
    stuck_limit = min(45.0, max(2.0, 0.25*time_limit))
    check = 1
    lr_base = 0.12
    tau_frac0 = 0.10? We need tune.
    while time<deadline:
        progress=(now-start)/time_limit
        # if no improve, perturb
        if now-last_improve > stuck_limit:
             # either noise current or restart from best with noise in Fourier
             if rng.random()<0.5:
                sigma=0.15
                v = v_best + rng.normal(...)? Keep v_best? We can maintain v_best from best_a.
             else: random Fourier modulation of best
             last_improve=now
             continue
        a=_soft(v)
        F=fft(a,m)
        b=ifft(F*F).real[:L]
        bmax=max(b)
        # choose tau
        tau_frac = 0.15*(1-progress)+0.02
        if rng.random()<0.3: tau_frac *= rng.uniform(0.5,2.0)
        tau = max(1e-12, bmax*tau_frac)
        x=(b-bmax)/tau; x=clip; w=softmax
        # add target gradient if target not None? maybe alternate: every 50 iters do target if target available
        if target is not None and t % 20 == 0:
             w_target = (b - target) # target area1
             # scale target weights to comparable with w
             w_scale = np.linalg.norm(w) / max(np.linalg.norm(w_target),1e-30)
             w = 0.5*w + 0.5*w_target*w_scale
        else:
             # topk bias
             top_k=...; idx; w[idx]+=0.3; w/=sum
        W=fft(w,m)
        c=ifft(W*np.conj(F)).real[:n]
        # optional floor c? no
        cm=np.dot(a,c)
        gv=a*(c-cm)
        grms=np.sqrt(np.mean(gv*gv))
        if not finite or <1e-14: perturb
        gv/=grms
        # Adam
        t+=1; update
        lr = lr_base * (1 - 0.6*progress)
        step = mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
        step=clip(step,-0.6,0.6)
        v_new = v - lr*step
        # line search? maybe evaluate every check; if score improved update last.
        # To ensure descent, try multiple step multipliers if current worsens.
        an=_soft(v_new); sc=score_fft(an)
        if finite and sc < best_score*1.000? update best? But do we accept if not best? We need maintain trajectory. If not improving best but maybe progress, we can accept if sc < current score? We don't track current score exactly. We can evaluate current a occasionally.
```

Need manage trajectory. Simplest: Always take step, regardless score, unless invalid. Keep best. If stuck, perturb. Adam may drift. To improve, use **backtracking to best** if score degrades significantly. We can evaluate current score every `check` steps. Keep `cur_score` maybe approximate. We can also use **gradient sign with hard active set** every some iterations: choose weights uniform over lags where b > bmax*(1-0.02). This directly flattens top. Let's implement a function to produce weights:
```
def _make_weights(b, bmax, tau, top_frac, top_bonus, rng):
   x=(b-bmax)/tau; softmax
   top_k=max(1,int(top_frac*b.size))
   idx=argpartition(b,-top_k)[-top_k:]
   w[idx]+=top_bonus
   w/=sum
   return w
```
Tune top_frac 0.02-0.1, bonus 0.5-2.0. If top_bonus high, focus peaks. Could randomize each iter or anneal.

Let's implement a more deterministic adaptive weight:
- Use softmax with tau.
- Additionally add `bonus * exp((b - bmax)/tau2)` where tau2 smaller (e.g., tau*0.1) to emphasize max. This is natural: w = softmax((b-bmax)/tau) + bonus*softmax((b-bmax)/(tau*0.1)); renorm.
This avoids argpartition.

**Perturbation/restart strategies**:
- If no improvement for `stuck_time`, choose one of:
   a) v = v_best + sigma * rng.normal(size=n) (sigma maybe 0.05-0.2) and continue. This explores local.
   b) Modify best in frequency: take a_best, multiply by `1 + rho * cos(2π k x + φ)` for low k, renorm. Then v=log. rho ~0.1-0.3. This changes shape.
   c) Interpolate best with a random param init (or trapezoid spectral) and optimize.
- To avoid losing progress, always start perturbation from best, not current.

- Powers: a=(x+eps)^p and (1-x+eps)^p for p in [0.25,0.5,1,2,4,8], and product x^p (1-x)^q (Beta) for p/q positive/negative? Add small epsilon.
- Hyperbolic/sec/cosh: e^{-c*(x-0.5)^2} (bell), 1/cosh(c*(x-0.5)) (U/bell), maybe inverted.
- Sinusoidal nonnegative: for k in [1,2,3,4], a=1+ρ cos(2π k x+φ) with ρ up to 0.7 and phase; ensure nonneg by max.

x = arange(L)-center; ay=abs(x)/dmax  # 0 center to1 ends

```

`spectral_factor(target, a0, n, m, iters, blend, method='newton')` details:
```
L=len(target); T=fft(target, m)
a=_proj_nonneg_sum(a0); a_floor? use v? We'll work in a-space with blending.
F=fft(a,m)
best=a; best_sc=score
for it in range(iters):
   if method newton:
      # avoid division by zero
      F_safe = F + 1e-12j
      F_target = 0.5*(F_safe + T/F_safe)
      lam = blend? maybe 0.8
      F = (1-lam)*F + lam*F_target
   else phase:
      Fabs=np.abs(F)+1e-12
      phase=F/Fabs
      F_target=phase*np.sqrt(np.abs(T)+1e-12)
      F=(1-lam)*F+lam*F_target
   anew=ifft(F).real[:n]
   pos=np.maximum(anew,0)
   psum=pos.sum()
   if psum <=1e-6: pos=a.copy()
   # normalize pos to sum1
   pos/=psum
   # maybe apply endpoint taper? not if target does.
   a_new=(1-blend_step)*a + blend_step*pos
   a_new=np.maximum(a_new,1e-12)
   a_new/=sum
   score=score_fft(a_new)
   if score<best: best=a_new
   a=a_new
   # optional clamp if score gets worse significantly: a = (1-0.5*blend) old? 
return best,best_sc
```
Need `lam` (frequency blend) and `blend_step` (time domain). Use lam=0.7, blend=0.6. For Newton, F can blow if F small; maybe clip magnitude: `F=np.clip(F.real, -1e6,1e6)+...`; check finite; if fail break.
Because we use complex, `np.abs(T)` maybe zeros. Newton `T/F` can have large phases. Might converge to wrong root. Phase method perhaps more stable. We can run both methods with different random phase initial? Initialize F from a0. For nonnegative a, F phase has some structure. Newton can flip sign; phase preserves. Previous used Newton-like and worked. We can implement a hybrid: `F_target_sqrt = np.sqrt(T / (F + 1e-12))`? Actually if want F^2=T, target F = sqrt(T) but choose branch closest to current F: `Fc = F * np.sqrt(T * np.conj(F)) / np.abs(F)^2`? Not. Simpler: use `F = F * np.sqrt(T / (F*F + eps)) = sqrt(T)/?` This introduces phase. Could just use phase method; it only enforces magnitude, less direct but robust.

Let's test mentally: target trapezoid T. Phase method sets |F| fixed to sqrt(|T_hat|), keeps current phase. Inverse time signal alternates; nonnegative projection gradually shapes phase. This is common. Newton may be faster but can instability. We'll implement both and choose best.

Maybe include **cycle-spectral**: target magnitude and nonnegative time domain alternation (GC). This is `spectral_factor(method='gc')`:

   a = max(a,0); normalize
   # freq: set F=fft(a); F = phase(F)*sqrt(|T|); a=ifft(F).real

Dedup: compute L2 distance normalized; skip if close to existing (<0.03). But expensive if many; K small.

Now, optimization stages. Instead of separate optimize for each candidate, we can run a **global best optimizer** that can switch candidates. But simpler: top 4 candidates, allocate time slices. For each candidate, call `optimize_seq` with limit. After each, update best. At end of stage, final polish best with remaining time.

Need `optimize_seq` not too long setup. It uses line search; time limit maybe can be short. For short (<20s), line search overhead may limit steps. We can adjust: if time_limit < 30, use simpler no line search? Or still okay.

Let's implement `optimize_seq` with an `intensive` flag based on time.
Pseudo:
```
def optimize_seq(a0, time_limit, seed, target=None, verbose=False):
    n = len(a0)
    m = _next_pow2(2*n)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed+hash?)
    a = _proj_nonneg_sum(a0)
    v = _log_plus(a)
    v -= mean
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_fft(a)
    v_best=v.copy()
    mu=zeros; nu=zeros; t=0
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.95; eps=1e-8
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max(1,time_limit)
    last_imp=start
    stuck = min(max(5,0.3*time_limit),60)
    use_ls = time_limit > 30
    while now<deadline:
       progress = (now-start)/time_limit
       if now-last_imp > stuck:
           # restart from best with perturbation
           sigma = 0.1 + 0.25*(1-progress)
           if rng.random() < 0.4:
              v = v_best + rng.normal(0,sigma,n)
           else:
              a = _proj_nonneg_sum(best_a)
              k = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, min(8, n//16))))
              phi = rng.uniform(0,2π)
              x = np.linspace(0,1,n)
              window = 1 + sigma*cos(2π k x + phi)
              a = np.maximum(a*window,0); rename
              v = _log_plus(a) - mean
           v = clip
           last_imp=now
           # zero momentum? reset
           mu*=0.5; nu*=0.5
           continue
       a = _soft(v)
       F=fft(a,m); b=ifft(F*F).real[:L]; bmax=max
       cur_score = 2*n*bmax (if finite)
       if not finite: break
       # choose weights
       tau = bmax*(0.12*(1-progress)+0.015)
       tau *= rng.uniform(0.7,1.4) maybe
       if tau <=1e-300: tau=1e-300
       xw=(b-bmax)/tau; clip; w=exp(xw-xmax); w/=sum
       top_k=max(1,int(0.03*b.size))
       idx=argpartition(b,-top_k)[-top_k:]
       w[idx]+=0.6
       w/=sum
       # target mode? skip for now
       W=fft(w,m)
       c=ifft(W*np.conj(F)).real[:n]
       cm=np.dot(a,c)
       gv=a*(c-cm)
       grms=np.sqrt(np.mean(gv*gv))
       if not finite or grms<1e-14:
           # perturb and continue
           v = v + rng.normal(0,0.05,n); reset; continue
       gv/=grms
       t+=1
       mu=...
       mhat=...
       step=mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
       # center step? step may have nonzero weighted mean? Since gv centered, Adam may not exactly; project again:
       step = step - a*np.dot(step,a)  # remove constant nu
       step_max=np.max(np.abs(step)); if finite: step/=step_max? We want lr scale.
       # Actually if divide by step_max, lr becomes 0.1 etc. Let's not divide, Adam step magnitude ~1 because gv normalized. We can clip.
       step=clip(step,-1,1)
       lr = 0.12*(1-0.5*progress)
       if use_ls:
          accepted=False
          for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25):
              v_trial = v - mult*lr*step
              v_trial = v_trial - mean? If subtract mean, invariance. Do it.
              a_trial=_soft(v_trial)
              sc=score_fft(a_trial)
              if finite and sc <= cur_score + 1e-12:
                  v=v_trial; a=a_trial; cur_score=sc; accepted=True
                  if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                      best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy(); v_best=v.copy(); last_imp=now
                  break
          if not accepted:
              # maybe keep v but reduce momentum
              mu*=0.9; nu*=0.9
              # if cur_score > best_score*1.02, reset to best
              if cur_score > best_score*1.02:
                 v = v_best + rng.normal(0,0.05,n)
              continue
       else:
          v = v - lr*step; v-=mean; v=clip
          a=_soft(v)
          # score from next loop? compute maybe
          cur_score=score_fft(a)
          if sc<best...
    # final
    return _proj_nonneg_sum(best_a), best_score
```

Need careful: If we line search accept `sc <= cur_score`, but due current `cur_score` = score of v; step could be zero? If many no accept, stuck handled. We should also accept if `sc < best_score` even if >cur_score? No, to keep descent maybe not. But sometimes temporary increase can lead to better basin; line search prevents. Stuck perturb handles escapes.

If trial is worse than cur but much better than best? cur could be worse than best due drift; we may want to reset. We can track if `cur_score > best_score*(1+0.03)`, reset to best earlier.

Issue: We recompute `a=_soft(v)` at loop start; if line search accepted, we set v,a. Next loop recomputes FFT, okay.

We need `score_fft` uses own m next pow2; to avoid reassign, we can implement `_score_from_F` with given m. For trial, since m same, compute directly.

Optimization of `softmax` weights: computing argpartition every iter O(L log k) maybe okay; for L=16383 every iter could be costly. We can skip top_k and use only softmax with sharper tau. Or top_k every 5 iter. Let's use weighting without argpartition:
```
w = exp((b-bmax)/tau)
w = w + bonus * exp((b-bmax)/(tau*0.25))
w/=sum
```
This emphasizes peaks. No sort. We can tune bonus. Or use `w = w ** sharp`? Since b nonnegative. Let's do:
```
s1=(b-bmax)/tau
s2=(b-bmax)/(0.1*tau)
w = (np.exp(clip(s1,-50,50)) + 0.5*np.exp(clip(s2,-50,50))) / sum
```
This is efficient. Top lags with near max get boosted. We can randomize `sharp_bonus` 0.2-1.0.

Alternatively, if bmax has multiple equal, both exp peak. Good.

Use `tau` relative to bmax. For n large, bmax~0.0001; tau~1e-5. exp of (small differences)/tau. Good.

**Adam beta2**: If beta2=0.95, vhat responsive. Use beta1=0.9. eps 1e-8.

**Learning rate**: Since gv normalized to RMS1, Adam step ~1. lr 0.1 in log space is moderate. Could be 0.2 early. We can use `lr = 0.18*(1-0.6*progress)+0.02`. Step clipped 0.8. Try line search.

If we divide gv by grms, for large n, gradient components may be small? RMS1 scaling means step size independent. Good.

In a discrete objective, high freq may reduce score? Maybe. But smoothing can help. We can add optional `smooth_v(v, lam)`: v = v - lam * (v - roll?) Actually subtract second derivative: v -= alpha*(v[:-2]-2v[1:-1]+v[2:])? Could be overhead. Maybe skip.

**End point floor**: Ensure a positive in log. `_log_plus` with min 1e-12. If a has zeros, log -27; soft can recover.

**Score with FFT and negative small b**: b from ifft may have tiny negatives; use max(0,b). Max score affected negligibly. For exact, nonnegative a => b nonnegative mathematically, numerical okay. Use `np.maximum(b,0,out=b)`.

Let's design `spectral_factor` more concretely and test possible pitfalls.
- Input target T length L=2n-1, max1.
- T=fft(T,m). T may have small imaginary due real; okay.

- Frequency blend λ: We can use 0.5 + 0.5*sin? Start 0.8, decay to0.4? Use `lam = 0.9 - 0.4*it/iters`.
- In Newton, `F_update = 0.5*(F + T/F)` is unnormalized; if F magnitude large/small. To avoid mag drift, normalize F magnitude? In GC, frequency magnitude target. Let's focus on GC/phase retrieval; it naturally maintains magnitude. Newton may not. But previous spectral used Newton and small time. We can implement `spectral_gc`:
```
F=fft(a,m)
Tmag=sqrt(abs(fft(target,m)))
a_best=a; sc=score
for it:
   # frequency projection
   F=fft(a,m)
   Fabs=abs(F)+1e-15
   F = F/Fabs * Tmag
   # maybe add small previous phase? already.
   anew=ifft(F).real[:n]
   # time projection

   if pos.sum() <= 1e-10: # fail
       break
   pos/=pos.sum()

   sc=score; if sc<best update
return best
```
This alternates exact frequency magnitude and nonnegative. It tends to find a with convolution close to target? Target T real; its FFT magnitude plus phase. Enforcing |F|=sqrt(|T|) means |F^2|=|T|, but phase of F^2 not constrained. In time projection, phase evolves. It's standard for power spectrum (|F|^2) not square. Here we need F^2, magnitude same but phase doubled. The phase projection may not enforce correct phase of T, but nonneg time may. Could work.

Newton method enforces phase: solves F^2=T. Let's implement a stable Newton with magnitude normalization:
```
F_t = fft(target,m)
F = fft(a,m)
for it:
    F_safe = F + 1e-20

    # Apply to time, nonneg project, re FFT.

```

But can GC find trapezoid target? It enforces power spectrum |F|^2=|T|. Since T is real convolution target, F^2=T => |F|^2=sqrt(|T|?) Wait magnitude: |F^2|=|T|, so |F|=sqrt(|T|). Yes. For uniform triangular target, T_hat=sinc^2 positive, sqrt=sinc, phase uniform? GC can recover uniform. For trapezoid, if T_hat has negative regions, |T| ignores sign. The correct F^2 phase includes sign of T_hat (π where negative). Ignoring sign may fail. Newton uses sign via complex T. Maybe we need complex phase.

Can use **phase retrieval with complex target**: At frequency step, set F to the square root branch of T closest to current F:
`F_target = F * np.sqrt(T * np.conj(F)) / np.abs(F)^2`? Let's derive: We want update F_new such that its square has phase and magnitude of T, while phase close to current. Let current F has phase φ. T has phase θ. Desired F phase = θ/2 (or +π/2). The closest to φ is choose sign. We can compute desired = sqrt(T) using complex sqrt; but branch cut may flip. We can choose sign to maximize Re(desired*conj(F)). That is robust:
```
D = np.sqrt(T + 0j)
# D has one branch; multiply by ±1
best_candidate = D
alt = -D
# choose elementwise sign to be close to F: sign = where(real(D*conj(F)) >=0, 1, -1)
D = sign * D
# Also scale magnitude? sqrt(|T|) already.
F_new = lam*F + (1-lam)*D
```
This enforces both magnitude and phase of T, with local branch choice per frequency. This is more direct than Newton. Then inverse, nonneg. This is excellent.

Implement `spectral_phase_target`:
```
T = fft(target,m)
D = np.sqrt(T)  # complex principal
for it:
    F = fft(a,m)
    # choose branch to align with F
    align = D * np.conj(F)
    sign = np.where(align.real >= 0.0, 1.0+0j, -1.0+0j)
    D_aligned = sign * D
    # Optional: blend D with F to avoid abrupt if D has zeros

def _add_unique(cands, scores, a, thresh=0.05):

Detailed `clip_spectral`:
```
def clip_spectral(a0, time_limit, seed, target_score=None, verbose=False):
    a=proj; best_score=score
    m=next_pow2(2*n)
    start; deadline
    rng
    while now<deadline:
       F=fft(a); b=ifft(F*F); bmax; cur_score
       if cur_score<best: update
       # desired max from score_goal? If target_score None, use 1.49
       goal_score = target_score if not None else 1.49
       goal_bmax = goal_score/(2*n)
       if bmax <= goal_bmax*1.001:
           # maybe break or reduce with tiny
           if best_score <= 1.503: break? continue small
       # Choose new M
       # reduce fraction adaptive: if far, reduce more? But too aggressive fail.
       frac = 0.97 if bmax/goal_bmax >1.1 else 0.995 if >1.02 else 0.999
       M_new = bmax*frac
       if M_new < goal_bmax*0.98: M_new = goal_bmax*1.005? Don't go below goal too much
       T = b.copy(); np.minimum(T,M_new,out=T)
       Tsum=sum(T) (should <=1); normalize T/Tsum (area1)
       # Ensure T max = M_new/Tsum? Fine.
       # Spectral project T with limited iters, starting from a
       a2,sc2 = spectral_factor(T, a, n, m, iters=25, blend=0.6, lam=0.8, time_limit=remaining small?)
       # Also try a few gradient peak steps on a2 to polish.
       a3,sc3 = optimize_seq(a2, min(5s, remaining...), seed,...) # maybe expensive
       if sc2<best_score: a=a2; best=...
       else:
          # if spectral failed, do one peak step or reduce frac less
          a = a2 if sc2 < cur_score*(1+0.01) else a # maybe keep old to avoid drift
       # If no improvement many iterations, perturb T or restart
```
This could be strong: It lowers target peak each outer and uses spectral to realize. But spectral from arbitrary target T (which is a valid current b clipped) may be closer feasible. It may converge.

However, `spectral_factor` as GC with target T may not reduce score monotonically; if not, we should not update and maybe stop. We can choose M_new closer to current (e.g., 0.999) to ensure small improvement. The loop can slowly lower.

Let's implement `clip_spectral` robustly:
- Use `spectral_factor` with `iters` maybe 30, `time_limit` inside? We can pass a time slice per outer: e.g., `outer_time = min(5s, remaining/50)`. But spectral_factor fixed iters; for large n 30 iters maybe 0.1s. OK.

Let's design stage:
1. Generate candidates (param + trapezoid spectral) and take best initial.
2. Run `clip_spectral` on best for a large fraction (e.g., 60% of stage).
3. Run `optimize_seq` on its result for remaining 40% (and maybe restarts).
This can exploit both.

But `clip_spectral` itself can loop until time; we can give it e.g., 50% and then polish. It updates best. Then `optimize_seq` from best.

Can `clip_spectral` get stuck if spectral fails? We can include fallback: if no improvement for `stuck_clip` (e.g., 30s), change reduction strategy: use trapezoid target (desired area) instead of current clipped; or do stochastic perturbation; or break to gradient. We'll implement.

Pseudo `clip_spectral`:
```
def clip_spectral(a0, time_limit, seed, goal_score=1.50, verbose=True):
    a = proj; best=a; best_sc=score; best_sc_clip? 
    start; deadline; last_imp=start
    while now<deadline:
       if now-last_imp > min(60,0.3*limit):
           # try a fixed trapezoid target with area based on best_sc-0.01 or random
           for area in [2/(best_sc*0.995), 2/(best_sc*0.98), 1.45]:
              T = make target area normalized
              a2,sc2=spectral_factor(T, a,... iters=40)
              if sc2<best_sc: update; break
           if no improve:
              # perturb best and continue
              a = perturbed(best)
              last_imp=now
           continue
       cur_sc = score from a (compute b)
       if cur_sc < best_sc: update

           else: # try lower reduction
              pass
    return best,best_sc

`_param_inits` function:
```
def _param_inits(n, rng, prev_a):
    cands=[]
    if prev_a and len>0: cands.append(interp(prev_a,n))
    cands.append(ones)
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    for c in [0.5,1,1.7,3,5,8,12,18]:
       cands.append(proj(exp(c*x)))
       cands.append(proj(exp(-c*x)))
    # power
    for p in [0.2,0.5,1,2,4]:
       cands.append(proj((x+1e-6)**p))
       cands.append(proj((1-x+1e-6)**p))
    # beta
    for p,q in [(0.5,0.5),(0.5,1.5),(1.5,0.5),(0.2,2),(2,0.2)]:
       cands.append(proj((x+1e-6)**p*(1-x+1e-6)**q))
    # quadratic/linear
    for A in [-0.8,-0.4,0.3,0.6,0.9]:
       for B in [-0.3,0,0.3]:
           a=1 + A*(2*x-1) + B*(2*x-1)**2
           cands.append(proj(np.maximum(a,0)))
    # sinusoidal log densities
    K=min(8,n//8)
    for _ in range(6):
       v=rng.normal(0,0.4,K)+1e-3? Build sum:
       vals = np.ones(n)
       for k in range(1,K+1):
          amp = rng.uniform(0.1,0.8) / (k**0.5)
          phase = rng.uniform(0,2π)
          vals += amp*np.cos(2π*k*x+phase)
       vals=np.maximum(vals,1e-3)
       cands.append(proj(vals))
    # exponentiated Fourier with linear trend
    for _ in range(6):
       coeff = rng.normal(0, [0.5/(k**0.7) ...])
       v = np.zeros(n); v += 0.5*x + Σ cos/sin; a=exp(v)*small
       cands.append(proj(a))
    # modulated prev
    if prev:
       base=interp(prev,n)
       for _ in range(5):
          k=rng.integers(1, max(2, min(8,n//16)))
          φ=rng.uniform(0,2π)
          rho=rng.uniform(0.05,0.45)
          a=base*(1+rho*cos(2π*k*x+φ)); cands.append(proj(max(a,0)))
    # dedup, score, sort? We can return raw list, stage will score.
    return cands[:30]
```
Keep not too many to score. For n=16384, many inits scoring each FFT O(n logn) maybe 30*0.01=0.3s, okay.

def _clip_spectral(a0, time_limit, seed, goal_score=1.49, donors=None):
    n=len; m=...
    a=proj; best=a; best_sc=score
    start; deadline; last_imp=start
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    # precompute? no
    while time<deadline:
       if now-last_imp > min(45,0.3*limit):
           # try fixed area target with desired area = 2/(max(goal_score, best_sc*0.98))? area target = 2/(best_sc*0.99)
           desired_score = max(goal_score, best_sc*0.985)
           area = 2.0/desired_score
           # generate 3 targets around area

              T = _target_with_area(n, area*uniform?) normalized

              if sc2 < best_sc: update; last_imp=now; break
           if no improve:
              # perturb best random or donor
              ...
           continue
       # compute b
       F=fft(a); b=ifft(F*F); b=max
       cur_sc=2n bmax
       if cur_sc < best_sc: best...
       goal_bmax = goal_score/(2n)
       if bmax <= goal_bmax * 1.001:
           # use fixed target area slightly below
           area = 2/(min(best_sc, 1.50)) * 1.01? maybe continue with trapezoid
       else:
           # fractional reduction
           ratio = bmax/goal_bmax
           if ratio > 1.5: frac=0.97
           elif ratio > 1.2: frac=0.985
           elif ratio > 1.05: frac=0.993
           else: frac=0.997
           M = bmax*frac
           T = np.minimum(b, M)
           # ensure target max normalized improves
           for _ in range(3):
               D = T.max()/T.sum()
               if D < bmax*(1 - 0.001) and D > goal_bmax*(0.99): break
               M *= 0.97

       # spectral
       t_it = 20 if n<=2048 else 15 if n<=8192 else 10? But time large; maybe 25.
       a2,sc2 = _spectral_factor(T, a, n, m, iters=25, freq_lam=0.8, time_blend=0.7)
       # accept if better than current or best
       if sc2 < cur_sc:
           a = a2
       elif sc2 < best_sc:
           a = a2
       else:
           # try blend current and a2
           blend=0.5
           ab = blend*sqrt(a*a2)??? Since a nonneg, geometric mean maybe.
           ab = proj((a*a2)**0.5)
           sc_ab=score_fft(ab)
           if sc_ab < cur_sc: a=ab

           else:
              # no accept, maybe reduce M more next loop; if no improvement count
              pass
       if best_sc < ... update last_imp
    return best,best_sc

Now, **`optimize_seq`** line search details more concretely.
We need a function `_score_from_F(a,F,m)` to avoid re-fft in trial? We'll implement.
```
def _score_from_F(a, F, m):
    n = a.size; L=2*n-1
    b = np.fft.ifft(F*F).real[:L]
    np.maximum(b,0,out=b)
    S = float(a.sum()) # should 1
    bmax = float(np.max(b)) if L else inf
    if not finite or S<=1e-300: return inf
    return float(2.0*n*bmax/(S*S))
```
For trial, a from soft sum1.

In `optimize_seq`, after computing F,b,bmax, cur_score. Compute weights:
```
tau = bmax*(0.15*(1.0-progress)+0.02)
if tau <= 0: tau=1e-300
diff=(b-bmax)/tau; clip(-60,60)
w1=np.exp(diff - diff.max())
s1=w1.sum(); if not finite w1=ones; else w1/=s1
diff2=diff/0.25
w2=np.exp(diff2 - diff2.max())
s2=w2.sum(); ...
bonus = 0.2 + 0.8*(1-progress)
w = w1 + bonus*w2; w/=w.sum()
```
This emphasizes top. For plateau, w2 also high on all plateau. Good.

Compute c:
```
W = fft(w, m)
c = ifft(W * np.conj(F)).real[:n]
```
`c` nonnegative? w positive, a positive, yes.
Gradient:
```
cm = np.dot(a,c)
gv = a * (c - cm)
grms = sqrt(mean(gv*gv))

```

# step should be gradient direction (positive where increase score). We want descend: v -= lr*step.
# remove mean to maintain sum: step = step - a*np.dot(step,a) (constant in v)
step = step - a*np.dot(step,a)
maxstep = max(abs(step)); if maxstep>0: step = step/maxstep? If we normalize step, lr larger. Let's not normalize; but gv RMS1, Adam step RMS~1. step magnitude maybe up to few. Clip to [-0.5,0.5]? If we normalize, lr is effective max log change. Let's normalize to max abs =1 for predictable line search.
maxstep = np.max(np.abs(step))
if maxstep > 0: step = step / maxstep
lr = 0.12 + 0.10*(1-progress) # 0.22 early to0.12 late
```

Pseudo:
```
def _spectral_factor(target, a0, n, m, iters=30, freq_lam=0.8, time_blend=0.7):
    T = np.fft.fft(target, m)
    D = np.sqrt(T + 0j)
    if not all finite: return proj(a0), score
    a = _proj_nonneg_sum(a0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
    a = a / a.sum()
    best = a.copy(); best_sc = score_fft(a)
    for it in range(iters):
        F = np.fft.fft(a, m)
        # align D
        align = np.real(D * np.conj(F))
        sign = np.where(align >= 0.0, 1.0, -1.0)
        D2 = sign * D
        # blend lam maybe anneal
        lam = freq_lam * (1.0 - 0.3*it/max(1,iters))
        Fnew = (1.0-lam)*F + lam*D2
        # if any nonfinite, break
        anew = np.fft.ifft(Fnew).real[:n]
        pos = np.maximum(anew, 0.0)
        ps = float(pos.sum())
        if not finite or ps < 1e-8:
            # fail: try small damping lam=0? break
            if it>3: break
            else: continue
        pos /= ps
        anew = (1.0-time_blend)*a + time_blend*pos
        anew = np.maximum(anew, 1e-12)
        anew /= anew.sum()
        sc = score_fft(anew)
        if finite and sc<best_sc: best=anew.copy; best_sc=sc
        a = anew
        if it % 5 ==0: print? optional
    return best, best_sc
```
Need `score_fft` inside each iter recomputes m; could be overhead. We can use `_score_from_F` after Fnew? But after time normalization, actual a changed. Could compute F=fft(a) after normalization; not too bad. For speed, implement `_score(a,m)` with precomputed m to avoid next_pow2.
```
def _score_m(a,m): F=fft(a,m); return _score_from_F...

def _target_with_area(n, area, power=1):
    area = min(max(area,1.0),2.0)
    if area <=1.0+1e-9: h=0
    elif power==1:
       h=area-1
    else:
       # continuous area=2h + 2(1-h)/(power+1)
       h = (area - 2/(power+1)) * (power+1) / (2 - 2/(power+1))? Derive: A=2h + 2(1-h)/(p+1) = 2/(p+1) + 2h(1-1/(p+1)) = 2/(p+1) + 2h p/(p+1). h=(A-2/(p+1))*(p+1)/(2p)
    h=clip(h,0,1-1e-6)
    return _target_coefficients(n,h,power)
```
For power=1, formula gives A=2/(2)+2h*1/2=1+h, ok. For power=2, A=2/3 + 4h/3.

def _process_stage(n, deadline, prev_a, rng):
   start=time.monotonic(); budget=max(5, deadline-now)
   spec_budget = min(45.0, 0.15*budget)
   cands = _generate_candidates(n, prev_a, rng, spec_budget, deadline)
   if not cands: cands=[prev_a]
   # sort/dedup top K=4
   cands = _score_dedup(cands, max_keep=4)
   if budget < 30:
       a_opt, sc = optimize_seq(cands[0], budget, int(rng.integers(...)))
       return best among cands? combine
   # clip on best candidate
   clip_time = min(0.35*budget, max(20, budget*0.35))
   a_clip, sc_clip = _clip_spectral(cands[0], clip_time, seed, goal_score=1.50)
   stage_best = a_clip if sc_clip < cands[0].score else cands[0]
   # optimize each candidate with remaining time slices, maybe include clip best
   candidates_opt = [stage_best] + cands[1:]
   weights = [0.5,0.2,0.15,0.15]
   opt_deadline = deadline - 5
   total_rem = max(0, opt_deadline-now)
   for i,cand in enumerate(candidates_opt):
       alloc=total_rem*weights[i]
       if alloc < 10: continue
       a_opt, sc_opt = optimize_seq(cand, alloc, seed, donors=cands[1:4])
       update stage_best
   # final polish stage_best with remaining
   rem=opt_deadline-now
   if rem>10:
       a_opt, sc_opt=optimize_seq(stage_best, rem, seed)
       update
   return stage_best, score
```

def _score_dedup(arrs, max_keep=6, thresh=0.05):
    out=[]; scores=[]
    for a in arrs:
       a=proj; sc=score_fft(a)
       if not finite: continue
       # check unique
       dup=False
       for b in out:
          if np.linalg.norm(a-b) < thresh:
             if sc < scores[idx]: update? Simpler skip
             dup=True; break
       if not dup:
          out.append(a); scores.append(sc)
    sort by scores
    return out[:max_keep]
```

Now code details:

```
def _proj_nonneg_sum(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
       return np.ones(1)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
       return a / a.size
    return a / s
```

`_soft`:
```
def _soft(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = float(np.max(v))
    if not np.isfinite(m): return ones
    x = v - m
    x = np.clip(x, -745.0, 700.0)
    e=np.exp(x); s=e.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: ones
    return e/s
```

`_log_plus`: `np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))`.

`_score_m(a,m)`:
```
def _score_m(a, m):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S<=1e-300: return inf
    F=np.fft.fft(a,m)
    b=np.fft.ifft(F*F).real[:2*n-1]
    np.maximum(b,0.0,out=b)
    bm=float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    if not finite or bm<0: return inf
    return 2.0*n*bm/(S*S)
```
For speed in optimize, we already have F. Implement `_score_from_F` too.

`_score_from_F(a,F,m)`:
```
    b=ifft(F*F).real[:L]; max; S=float(a.sum()); return ...
```

`score_fft(a)`: m=next_pow2; _score_m.

```

`_make_param_inits`: Need avoid too many for large n? We can limit random Fourier count based on n. Let's implement with deterministic lists and few random.

```
def _make_param_inits(n, rng, prev_a):
    c=[]
    if prev_a is not None: c.append(_interp(prev_a,n))
    c.append(np.ones(n)/n)
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    for cc in (0.3,0.7,1.3,2.0,3.5,6.0,10.0,16.0):
       c.append(_proj_nonneg_sum(np.exp(cc*x)))
       c.append(_proj_nonneg_sum(np.exp(-cc*x)))
    for p in (0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0):
       c.append(_proj_nonneg_sum((x+1e-6)**p))
       c.append(_proj_nonneg_sum((1-x+1e-6)**p))
    for p,q in ((0.5,0.5),(0.25,1.0),(1.0,0.25),(0.1,0.5),(0.5,0.1)):
       c.append(_proj_nonneg_sum((x+1e-6)**p*(1-x+1e-6)**q))
    # polynomials
    for A in (-0.9,-0.5,-0.2,0.2,0.5,0.9):
       for B in (-0.4,0.0,0.4):
           c.append(_proj_nonneg_sum(np.maximum(1.0 + A*(2*x-1) + B*(2*x-1)**2, 0.0)))
    # random log densities
    K = min(10, max(2, n//64))
    for _ in range(8):
       v = np.ones(n)
       trend = rng.normal(0,0.5)
       v += trend*x
       for k in range(1,K+1):
           amp = rng.uniform(0.2,1.0)/(k**0.6)
           ph = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
           v += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x + ph)
       v = np.maximum(v,1e-3)
       c.append(_proj_nonneg_sum(v))
    # exponentiated random Fourier
    for _ in range(6):
       v = np.zeros(n)
       v += rng.normal(0,0.4)*x
       for k in range(1,K+1):
           amp = rng.uniform(0.05,0.8)/(k**0.7)
           ph = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
           v += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x + ph)
           v += amp*rng.uniform(0,1)*np.sin(2*np.pi*k*x + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
       c.append(_proj_nonneg_sum(np.exp(v + 0.1*x)))
    # prev modulated
    if prev_a is not None:
       base=_proj_nonneg_sum(_interp(prev_a,n))
       for _ in range(6):
           k = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, min(8, n//16))))
           ph = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
           rho = rng.uniform(0.05,0.5)
           mod = 1.0 + rho*np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x + ph)
           c.append(_proj_nonneg_sum(np.maximum(base*mod,0.0)))
    return c
```
This may produce 100 inits; scoring/dedup top few. For n=16384, random arrays many but okay? 100*FFT maybe too much. We can limit: after param inits, if n>8192 reduce random loops. Simpler: generate max 40. We can append and if len>60 break. Or in dedup, score all until time? Maybe too many. Let's cap list to 40:

def _add_spectral_candidates(cands, n, prev_a, m, budget, rng):
    now=time.monotonic()
    base_a=None

    # maybe use best candidate in cands scored? We don't know scores. Use base_a and ones.

    for h,power in targets:
       if time.monotonic() > now+budget: break

       a2,sc2 = _spectral_factor(T, base_a, n, m, iters=iters, freq_lam=0.85, time_blend=0.7)
       cands.append(a2)

          cands.append(a3)

Implement `_score_normalize_list(arrays, max_keep=None)`:
```
def _score_normalize_list(arrays, max_keep=None, thresh=0.04):
    scored=[]; seen=[]
    for a in arrays:
       a=_proj_nonneg_sum(a)
       sc=score_fft(a)
       if not finite: continue
       # unique
       dup=False
       for b in seen:
           d=float(np.linalg.norm(a-b))
           if d < thresh:
              dup=True; break
       if not dup:
           scored.append((sc,a)); seen.append(a)
    scored.sort(key=_pair_score)
    if max_keep: scored=scored[:max_keep]
    return scored
```
This sorts by score. Use max_keep for top K. If we want all spectral after, we can score raw with max_keep=1 to get best, then generate more. But adding spectral after and rescore.

**Be cautious with memory/time in `_score_normalize_list` for many arrays**: It keeps seen arrays for dedup up to maybe 50; distance O(K^2 n) but K small (50*50*8192 ~20M) okay. For 16384, 50M okay.

Now, `_process_stage` code:
```
def _process_stage(n, deadline, prev_a, rng):
    now=time.monotonic()
    if now>=deadline: return prev_a, score_fft(prev_a) if prev_a is not None else inf
    budget = max(5.0, deadline-now)
    # generate param inits
    inits = _make_param_inits(n, rng, prev_a)
    # quick score to get best param
    scored_raw = _score_normalize_list(inits, max_keep=1)
    if not scored_raw:
       base = _proj_nonneg_sum(_interp(prev_a,n) if prev_a is not None else np.ones(n))
       return base, score_fft(base)
    cands = [scored_raw[0][1]]
    best_score = scored_raw[0][0]
    # spectral budget
    spec_deadline = min(deadline, now + min(60.0, 0.18*budget))
    m = _next_pow2(2*n-1)
    spec_cands = _add_spectral_candidates(cands, n, cands[0], m, spec_deadline, rng)
    cands = _score_normalize_list(cands, max_keep=5)
    # run optimization
    stage_best = cands[0][1]; stage_score=cands[0][0]
    # clip spectral on best candidate
    clip_budget = min(max(10.0, 0.30*budget), max(10.0, budget*0.30))
    if budget > 40:
       a_clip, sc_clip = _clip_spectral(stage_best, clip_budget, int(rng.integers(0,2**31-1)), goal_score=1.495)
       if sc_clip < stage_score: stage_best=a_clip; stage_score=sc_clip
    # optimizer slices
    opt_deadline = deadline - 5.0
    rem_total = max(0, opt_deadline - time.monotonic())
    if rem_total > 20:
       # Prepare candidate list: best plus top others
       opt_cands = [stage_best]
       for sc,a in cands:
          if np.linalg.norm(a-stage_best)>0.03: opt_cands.append(a)
          if len(opt_cands)>=4: break
       weights = (0.45,0.20,0.17,0.18)
       for i,cand in enumerate(opt_cands):
          if time.monotonic() >= opt_deadline - 10: break
          alloc = rem_total*weights[i] if i<len(weights) else rem_total*0.15
          if alloc < 8: continue
          seed = int(rng.integers(0,2**31-1))
          a_opt, sc_opt = optimize_seq(cand, alloc, seed, donors=opt_cands if len(opt_cands)>1 else None)
          if sc_opt < stage_score:
             stage_best=a_opt; stage_score=sc_opt
       # final polish
       rem = max(0, opt_deadline - time.monotonic())
       if rem > 10:
          seed = int(rng.integers(0,2**31-1))
          a_opt, sc_opt = optimize_seq(stage_best, rem, seed)
          if sc_opt < stage_score: update
    # ensure best
    if stage_best is None: stage_best=_proj_nonneg_sum(_interp(prev_a,n)...)
    return stage_best, _score_m(stage_best, m)
```
If `cands` after spectral has stage_best; good.

Now, **`_clip_spectral` fixed target stuck**: Need helper `_target_with_area`. Let's implement:
```
def _target_coefficients(n,h,power):
    L=2*n-1; c=n-1; d=c if c>0 else 1
    ay=np.abs(np.arange(L)-float(c))/float(d)
    h=float(np.clip(h,0.0,1.0-1e-9))
    T=np.ones(L)
    mask=ay>h
    if np.any(mask):
       t=(ay[mask]-h)/(1.0-h)
       t=np.clip(t,0,1)
       if power==1.0: T[mask]=1.0-t
       else: T[mask]=(1.0-t)**power
    T=np.maximum(T,0)
    if T.size>0: T[0]=0.0; T[-1]=0.0
    return T
```
If h=0, mask all, t=ay, T=1-ay triangular with endpoints0. Good.
If h=1, mask none; but we set endpoints0, area slightly <2. But h max 1-1e-9.

`_target_with_area(n, area, power)`:

area = float(np.clip(area,1.0,2.0))
if power==1.0:
  h = area - 1.0
else:
  # A = 2h + 2(1-h)/(p+1)
  p = max(1.1, float(power))
  h = ((area - 2.0/(p+1.0)) * (p+1.0)) / (2.0*p)
h=clip(h,0,1-1e-8)
T=_target_coefficients...
# refine h maybe by linear scaling? We can adjust T scale? Since area approximate. If desired area, we can compute actual sum and then modify h? For simplicity, return T. If area mismatch, normalized max changes. To improve, do a few bisection? maybe not.
return T / T.sum()  # area1
```

M = bmax*frac
for _ in range(5):
   T = np.minimum(b, M)
   s = T.sum()
   if s <=0: M *=0.9; continue
   D = T.max()/s
   if D < bmax*(1-0.002) and D > goal_bmax*0.98: break
   # If D too high, lower M further; if D too low, raise M maybe.
   if D >= bmax*(1-0.002): M *=0.95
   else: M *=1.1
return T/s
```

Let's write `_clip_spectral` code carefully:
```
def _clip_spectral(a0, time_limit, seed, goal_score=1.495):
    a=_proj_nonneg_sum(a0); n=a.size; m=next_pow2(2n-1); rng=default_rng(seed)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=score
    start; deadline; last_imp=start; no_imp_count=0
    while now<deadline:
        if now - last_imp > min(60.0, max(10.0, 0.3*time_limit)):
            # fixed targets
            tried=False
            for power in (1.0,2.0):
               if tried: break
               area = 2.0 / (min(best_sc, 1.52) * 0.99)
               # maybe areas slightly below
               for area_factor in (0.99,0.98,1.0):
                   T=_target_with_area(n, area*area_factor, power)
                   a2,sc2=_spectral_factor(T, a, n, m, iters=35, freq_lam=0.85, time_blend=0.7)
                   if sc2 < best_sc:
                      best=a2; best_sc=sc2; a=a2; last_imp=now; tried=True; break
            if not tried:
                # perturb best
                a, v? We work in a-space only. Perturb a:
                aa=_proj_nonneg_sum(best)
                rho=0.05+0.3*(progress)
                k=...; x=add; a=proj(max(aa*(1+rho cos));
                last_imp=now
            continue
        F=fft(a); b=ifft; bmax; cur_sc=...
        if cur_sc < best_sc: update best, a? a remains current, last_imp=now
        goal_bmax=goal_score/(2n)
        # choose M
        if bmax <= goal_bmax*1.001:
            # fixed target area for current best-0.002
            area = 2.0 / max(1.4, best_sc*0.995)
            T=_target_with_area(n, area, 1.0)
        else:
            ratio=bmax/goal_bmax
            if ratio>1.5: frac=0.97
            elif ratio>1.2: frac=0.985
            elif ratio>1.05: frac=0.993
            else: frac=0.997
            M=bmax*frac
            # enforce M not below goal
            T=np.minimum(b,M); s=T.sum()
            for _ in range(4):
                if s>0:
                   D=T.max()/s
                else: D=np.inf
                if np.isfinite(D) and D < bmax*(1-0.001): break
                M *=0.95
                if M <= goal_bmax: M=goal_bmax
                T=np.minimum(b,M); s=T.sum()
            if s<=0 or not finite: T=_target_with_area(n, 2.0/max(1.4,best_sc*0.995),1.0)
            else: T=T/s
        # spectral target T
        iters = 20 if n>8192 else 25 if n>2048 else 30
        a2,sc2=_spectral_factor(T, a, n, m, iters=iters, freq_lam=0.85, time_blend=0.7)
        # accept candidates
        accepted=False
        if sc2 < cur_sc:
            a=a2; accepted=True
            if sc2 < best_sc: best=a2.copy; best_sc=sc2; last_imp=now
        else:
            # try blends
            for blend in (0.5,0.7,0.3):
               ab=blend*a + (1-blend)*a2 # or geometric? Use arithmetic and geometric
               # ensure sum1
               ab=_proj_nonneg_sum(ab)
               sc_ab=score_fft(ab)
               if sc_ab < cur_sc:
                  a=ab; accepted=True
                  if sc_ab < best_sc: best=ab.copy; best_sc=sc_ab; last_imp=now
                  break
            if not accepted:
               # try geometric
               ag=_proj_nonneg_sum(np.sqrt(np.maximum(a*a2,0.0)))
               # score geometric maybe expensive; optional
               if score_fft(ag) < cur_sc: ...
        # if best improved? no;
        # If no accept and cur_sc worse than best*1.02, reset a to best
        if not accepted and cur_sc > best_sc*1.02:
            a=best.copy()
    return best, best_sc
```
This uses a-space, no v. Good.

Let's now think about **possible failure due to complex sqrt sign per frequency**. D sign alignment elementwise can produce a phase that is discontinuous, causing time-domain high-frequency oscillations. Nonneg projection smooths. We can also use **low-pass smoothing of D phase** to improve? Maybe not.

Alternatively, Newton `F_new=0.5(F+T/F)` might produce smoother phase. Could include in `_spectral_factor` a `method` parameter and run both? To save time, use GC (square root) only. We can add an optional Newton step in `_spectral_factor` if `method`? But candidate generation can call with different method to explore. Let's implement `_spectral_factor` with `mode` string:
- 'sqrt': as above.
- 'newton': Fnew=0.5*(F+T/F) (safe), then time projection.
- 'gc': phase magnitude.
But code length. Maybe implement both in one? We can use sqrt and also every few iterations maybe Newton. Simpler, stick with sqrt. It should be good.

Let's verify sqrt target area1: T_hat[0]=1. D[0]=1. If F[0]=1 (sum a=1 after normalization), alignment sign keeps 1. Good. For T normalized area1, D magnitude at high freq ≤1. The desired F energy? Parseval: sum |F|^2/m = sum |a|^2. The target's desired |F|^2 average = mean |D|^2 = mean |T_hat|/m, which equals sum T^2 /? Not constrained. Fine.

Detailed code for `_score_normalize_list`: It uses `score_fft`, which computes m each call. For many candidate scoring, okay.
```
def _score_normalize_list(arrs, max_keep=None, thresh=0.04):
    scored=[]; seen=[]
    limit = 80
    for a in arrs:
       if len(scored)>=limit: break
       try:
          a=n a; sc=score_fft
       except: continue
       if not np.isfinite(sc): continue
       dup=False
       for b in seen:
          d = float(np.linalg.norm(a-b))
          if d < thresh:
             dup=True; break
       if not dup:
          scored.append((sc,a)); seen.append(a.copy())
    scored.sort(key=_pair_score)
    if max_keep is not None and len(scored)>max_keep:
       scored=scored[:max_keep]
    return scored
```
Tuple (sc,a); sort key returns sc. Good. If two arrays close and one lower score appears later, we skip; may miss better. We can if dup and sc lower update: need index tracking. But not critical. Could implement:

def _add_spectral_candidates(cands, base_a, n, m, spec_deadline, rng):
    areas = [(1.25,1.0),(1.33,1.0),(1.42,1.0),(1.55,1.0),(1.8,1.0),(1.38,2.0),(1.62,2.0)]
    iters = 35 if n<=1024 else 28 if n<=4096 else 20
    base = _proj_nonneg_sum(base_a)
    np.random? rng not used.
    for area,power in areas:
       if time.monotonic() >= spec_deadline: break
       T = _target_with_area(n, area, power)
       a2,sc2 = _spectral_factor(T, base, n, m, iters=iters, freq_lam=0.85, time_blend=0.7)
       cands.append(a2)
       # maybe try with base reversed if n small
       if n<=2048 and time.monotonic()<spec_deadline:
          a3,sc3=_spectral_factor(T, base[::-1], n, m, iters=iters, freq_lam=0.65, time_blend=0.5)
          cands.append(a3)
    return cands

Let's write optimize_seq code robust:
```
def optimize_seq(a0, time_limit, seed, donors=None, verbose=False):
    a0 = _proj_nonneg_sum(a0)
    n = int(a0.size)
    if n<=1: return a0, 2.0
    m = _next_pow2(2*n-1)
    L=2*n-1
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) % (2**31-1))
    a = a0.copy()
    v = _log_plus(a)
    v = v - float(np.mean(v))
    v = np.clip(v, -35.0, 35.0)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = _score_m(a, m)
    if not finite:
       best_a = np.ones(n)/n; v=...; best_score=2.0
    v_best = v.copy()
    mu=np.zeros(n); nu=np.zeros(n); t=0
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.95; eps=1e-8
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max(5.0,float(time_limit))
    last_imp=start
    x = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        now=time.monotonic(); progress=min(1.0,(now-start)/max(1e-9, deadline-start))
        stuck_limit = min(max(10.0, 0.30*time_limit), 60.0)
        if now-last_imp > stuck_limit:
            # reset
            if donors is not None and len(donors)>0 and rng.random()<0.4:
                donor = donors[int(rng.integers(0,len(donors)))]
                a = _proj_nonneg_sum(donor)
            else:
                a = _proj_nonneg_sum(best_a)
                k = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, min(12, n//16))))
                ph = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
                rho = 0.05 + 0.35*(1.0-progress)
                a = _proj_nonneg_sum(np.maximum(a*(1.0+rho*np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x+ph)),0.0))
            v = _log_plus(a) - mean; clip
            mu *=0.5; nu*=0.5; t=0
            last_imp=now
            continue
        # ensure a from v (in case reset)
        a = _soft(v)
        F = np.fft.fft(a, m)
        b = np.fft.ifft(F*F).real[:L]
        np.maximum(b,0, out=b)
        bmax = float(np.max(b))
        if not finite: break
        S = float(a.sum()) # 1
        cur_score = 2.0*n*bmax/(S*S)
        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score=cur_score; best_a=a.copy(); v_best=v.copy(); last_imp=now
        # weights
        tau = bmax * (0.18*(1.0-progress)+0.015)
        if not finite or tau <= 1e-300: tau = max(1e-300, bmax*0.05)
        d1 = (b-bmax)/tau
        d1 = np.clip(d1, -60.0, 60.0)
        d1 = d1 - float(np.max(d1))
        w1 = np.exp(d1); s1=float(w1.sum())
        if not finite or s1<=0: w1=np.ones(L)/L
        else: w1/=s1
        d2 = np.clip(d1/0.25, -60,60) - max
        w2=...
        bonus = 0.15 + 0.65*(1-progress)
        w = w1 + bonus*w2; s=float(w.sum()); if s>0 w/=s
        W=np.fft.fft(w,m)
        c=np.fft.ifft(W*np.conj(F)).real[:n]
        # c should be nonnegative, but numerical maybe small negative; don't max? If negative, gradient maybe. Use max(c,0)? For nonnegative weights, c nonnegative, but due FFT roundoff could -1e-18. Set max(c,0) to avoid issues.
        np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)
        cm=float(np.dot(a,c))
        gv=a*(c-cm)
        grms=float(np.sqrt(np.mean(gv*gv)))
        if not finite or grms < 1e-14:
             v = v + rng.normal(0, 0.05, n); v=clip; mu*=0.5; nu*=0.5; continue
        gv = gv / grms
        t += 1
        mu = beta1*mu + (1-beta1)*gv
        nu = beta2*nu + (1-beta2)*(gv*gv)
        b1 = beta1**t; b2 = beta2**t
        mhat = mu / max(1e-12, 1-b1)
        vhat = nu / max(1e-12, 1-b2)
        step = mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat)+eps)
        step = step - a*np.dot(step,a)
        max_step = float(np.max(np.abs(step)))
        if not finite or max_step <= 1e-15:
            v = v + rng.normal(0,0.05,n); clip; continue
        step = step / max_step
        lr = 0.18 + 0.10*(1.0-progress)  # early 0.28? Maybe 0.18+0.10=0.28. Clip line search.
        accepted=False
        # Try multipliers
        for mult in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125):
            v_trial = v - mult*lr*step
            v_trial = v_trial - float(np.mean(v_trial))
            v_trial = np.clip(v_trial, -35,35)
            a_trial = _soft(v_trial)
            sc_trial = _score_m(a_trial, m)
            if np.isfinite(sc_trial) and sc_trial <= cur_score + 1e-12:
                v = v_trial; a = a_trial; cur_score=sc_trial; accepted=True
                if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_score=cur_score; best_a=a.copy(); v_best=v.copy(); last_imp=now
                break
        if not accepted:
            mu *=0.8; nu*=0.8
            if cur_score > best_score*1.02:
                v = v_best.copy() + rng.normal(0,0.03,n)
                v = v - mean; clip
                a=_soft(v)
    # final ensure
    a = _soft(v)
    sc = _score_m(a,m)
    if finite and sc<best_score: best
    return _proj_nonneg_sum(best_a), float(best_score)
```
Learning rate maybe too high; line search reduces. Early lr 0.28 max log step; with step normalized max1, large. Could be okay but might oscillate. Use lr=0.12 + 0.08*(1-progress)=0.20 early. Let's set `lr = 0.14 + 0.08*(1.0-progress)` (0.22 early to0.14). Good.

Line search comparing to `cur_score`: current a's score. But if current score is not best (drift), accepting any lower than current is okay. However if current > best, line search may still accept but not best. If not accept, reset if current > best*1.02. Good.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    seed=int(seed); budget_s=float(max(60,budget_s))
    start = time.monotonic()
    global_deadline = start + max(60, budget_s - 30)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    prev = _load_previous()
    best_a = prev
    best_score = score_fft(prev) if prev is not None else np.inf
    if best_a is None:
       best_a = np.ones(1024)/1024; best_score=2.0
    print(...)
    base = max(60, budget_s-30)
    stage_defs = [(512,0.02),(1024,0.05),(2048,0.12),(4096,0.32),(8192,0.38),(16384,0.11)]
    for n, frac in stage_defs:
       now=time.monotonic()
       if now + 30 >= global_deadline: break
       alloc = base*frac
       stage_deadline = min(global_deadline - 5, now + alloc)
       # If best_score is already excellent and n > len(best_a)? still continue.
       prev_stage = _interp(best_a,n) if best_a is not None else np.ones(n)/n
       a_new, sc_new = _process_stage(n, stage_deadline, prev_stage, rng)
       if a_new is not None and np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_score:
           best_a=a_new; best_score=sc_new
       print stage
    # final polish remaining
    rem = max(0, global_deadline - time.monotonic())
    if best_a is not None and rem > 30:
       seed2=int(rng.integers(0,2**31-1))
       a_opt, sc_opt = optimize_seq(best_a, rem, seed2)
       if sc_opt < best_score: update
    # maybe if best length > 16384? no.
    # final exact validation print for length <=8192
    try:
       if len(best_a) <= 8192:
          exact = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in best_a])
          print('Final FFT score %.8f exact %.8f n=%d' % ...)
          # If exact much worse due numerical? no. If exact not finite, fallback to ones.
          if not np.isfinite(exact): best_a=np.ones(2048)/2048
       else:
          print('Final FFT score %.8f n=%d (exact omitted)' ...)
    except Exception as e:
       print('Exact validation skipped: %s' % e)
    return [float(x) for x in _proj_nonneg_sum(best_a)]
